  - *Eligibility:* Open; no student/startup criterion mentioned. Trial is time-limited (14 days), presumably for new accounts. Regions at signup: US East, Frankfurt, São Paulo, Singapore, Sydney (changeable later).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up via the 'Get started' / 'Try Metabase Cloud free' button, which routes to https://store.metabase.com/checkout; pick a hosting region at signup.
  - *Expiry:* Trial ends after 14 days; continued use requires conversion to a paid plan. No renewal of the free period is stated.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a TRIAL, not a free tier — there is no perpetual free Metabase Cloud plan. The official page does not state whether a credit card is required for the trial; checkout goes through a store, so card capture is plausible but unconfirmed. Usage-based extras after trial: AI (Metabase-hosted) $3.75/1M tokens with 1M tokens included; Transforms 1,000 runs included then $0.01/run (Advanced $0.02 BI / $0.01 EA); storage first 1M rows free then $2/1M rows. Data-residency limits: only the 5 listed regions; air-gapped or HIPAA needs go to self-hosted/Enterprise.
  - *Source:* https://www.metabase.com/pricing/(corroboratedbyhttps://www.metabase.com/cloudandhomepage)
- **Education / student program — does not exist** (medium) — No student or education license is offered. metabase.com/education and metabase.com/startups both return HTTP 404. The pricing page, homepage, cloud page, GitHub repo and blog index contain no education discount, .edu requirement, or student offer. The only 'free' paths are the perpetual OSS self-hosted edition and the 14-day Cloud trial.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — program not found; nothing to claim.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. Students use the same free OSS self-hosted edition (unlimited users) as anyone else.
  - *Expiry:* N/A.
  - *Gotchas:* Absence confirmed on the official site today, but 'no program' cannot be guaranteed if one were announced off-site; the free OSS edition is the de facto student offer (no .edu email needed, no seat caps — unlike typical SaaS student tiers that cap seats or features).
  - *Source:* https://www.metabase.com/(404sat/educationand/startups;homepage+pricing+blogscannedtoday)
- **2025–26 change:** No dedicated free Cloud tier with limited questions/users currently exists on the official site — the 'known so far' item (Metabase Cloud free tier, limited questions/users) appears to describe either the 14-day trial or a plan that was never a perpetual free tier; as of 2026-10-22 the free offerings are only (a) the unlimited self-hosted OSS/Community Edition and (b) the 14-day Cloud trial. No 2025-2026 sunset of a student or startup program was found: /education and /startups are 404 and no pricing-change or program-anniversary posts appear in the blog index (closest historical posts: 'Announcing Metabase Cloud' and 'An Enterprising Opening'). Caveat: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted, so third-party/press cross-checks of 2025-2026 changes (e.g., plan renames, tier limit tweaks) could not be done; confidence in absence-claims rests solely on the official pages fetched today. The OSS repo README describes the cloud offering strictly as a 'free trial', with no perpetual free tier.

### New Relic Free Tier

- **Perpetual Free Tier (all accounts; no .edu or startup requirement)** (high) — Free forever plan verified on newrelic.com/pricing as of 2026-10-22: 100 GB data ingest/month (account-wide, not per user), 1 full-platform user with access to all 50+ platform capabilities (APM with distributed tracing, infrastructure monitoring, error tracking, digital experience monitoring), unlimited basic users (view custom non-quickstart dashboards, query, alert), 500 synthetic checks/month, unlimited hosts/agents/containers/devices/AWS Fargate tasks/cloud functions, default data retention at least 8 days, default log obfuscation. Explicitly no data egress or hydration charges. Overage: if you exceed 100 GB in a month, data ingest ceases and you lose platform access until you upgrade or the 1st of the following month (auto re-enables). Paid contrast for context: Pro full user $349/user annual or $418.80/month; Core user $49; overage $0.40/GB standard or $0.60/GB 'Data Plus' add-on; EU data-center storage +$0.05/GB/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone: no .edu email, no startup age/funding cap, no credit card required ('No credit card required to start' per pricing FAQ), no stated region limits.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a free New Relic account at newrelic.com (no credit card). 2) Free tier is active immediately with the 100 GB/mo + 1 full user + 500 synthetic checks. 3) Overage suspends ingest until the 1st or an upgrade — never billed.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; no expiry date. Monthly limits reset on the 1st of each month.
  - *Gotchas:* Exceeding 100 GB halts ingest AND platform access for the month (not a bill, but you go dark). Only 1 full-platform user is free — team collaboration relies on unlimited basic users (restricted access). The '10,000 synthetic monitors' figure that appears in a pay-as-you-go FAQ is a different (paid-edition) benefit, not the free 500 checks. No separate time-boxed free trial is advertised — the perpetual tier is the trial replacement.
  - *Source:* https://newrelic.com/pricing
- **New Relic for Students (education program)** (high) — Verified on newrelic.com/students as of 2026-10-22: 500 GB data ingest per month and 3 full-platform users (vs. 100 GB / 1 user on the standard free tier), plus access to 30+ platform capabilities, a monthly newsletter, exclusive certifications/workshops, potential sponsorships, and office hours with New Relic staff. Benefits are gated behind linking an education partner account.
  - *Eligibility:* Students (and select technical-bootcamp students) at accredited colleges, universities, or technical schools; 'proof of current student status is required'. Two routes: (a) GitHub Education — students in the GitHub Student Developer Pack, verified via GitHub; (b) Platzi — open to Spanish/Portuguese-speaking learners in Latin America and beyond, but Platzi's side requires a monthly course subscription. Select bootcamps only; others email students@newrelic.com. No .edu-email-only shortcut is documented.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a free New Relic account (no credit card). 2) Have an active account with a partner: GitHub Education (verify student status in GitHub) or Platzi. 3) GitHub path: in New Relic go to Help icon → 'New Relic for Students' → 'GitHub Education' → 'Authorize with GitHub'. Platzi/other-partner path: submit the form with your 7-digit New Relic account number; benefits land in 5–10 business days. 4) Non-eligible bootcamp students email students@newrelic.com for manual verification.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page. No renewal date, cap, or grace period is published; presumably reverts to the standard 100 GB/month free tier if student status is lost.
  - *Gotchas:* Perks only activate after linking a partner account. Platzi route: the New Relic tier is free but Platzi itself costs a subscription. Graduating or losing student status has no documented re-enrollment or grace language — expect to drop to the base free tier. 3 full users is for 'collaborative projects' (class group work).
  - *Source:* https://newrelic.com/students
- **New Relic for Startups program** (medium) — Verified on newrelic.com/startups as of 2026-10-22: 5 full-platform users plus 'generous data ingest per month, free' — the exact GB figure is not published on the page. Full platform 'from day one — no gating, no tool switching' (AI Monitoring, APM, Logs), explicitly 'not just bankrolled with credits'; also includes a New Relic engineer alongside launch, office hours, and quarterly architecture reviews.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups; the page itself publishes NO startup-age cap, funding cap, headcount cap, or region limit — thresholds are applied during application review. No credit-card requirement mentioned (consistent with the no-CC free signup).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Submit the multi-step 'New Relic for Startups Registration' form on newrelic.com/startups. 2) 'Our team will reach out shortly, to process your application, and to confirm your place and next steps' — benefits are confirmed post-review rather than instantly granted.
  - *Expiry:* Not disclosed on the page (no program length, renewal terms, or end date).
  - *Gotchas:* Key numbers (monthly GB allotment, program duration, funding/age caps) are not public — the page is a marketing/registration funnel, so treat the exact benefit as 'confirmed after application'. Do not assume 100 GB: that figure on the site belongs to the standard free tier, not the startup program. If not accepted, you fall back to the perpetual free tier.
  - *Source:* https://newrelic.com/startups
- **2025–26 change:** No New Relic free offer was sunset or materially changed in 2025-2026 per pages fetched today (2026-10-22): the perpetual free tier (100 GB/month, 1 full user, unlimited basic users, 500 synthetic checks) is still advertised on newrelic.com/pricing, the site navigation still shows '100GB/month free' / 'New Relic Free Tier', the students program (500 GB/mo, 3 users) is live at newrelic.com/students, and the startups program is live at newrelic.com/startups. No separate time-boxed 'free trial' is currently advertised — the perpetual free tier with no credit card is the entry point. Note: WebSearch could not be run this session (search budget exhausted), so 2025-2026 change-verification relied on fetching official pages and the blog index rather than searching; the blog index (Oct 2026 posts) shows no pricing/plan sunset announcements. Also: New Relic has no free CDN offer at all (CDN is not a New Relic product); the 'Uptime' front maps to the 500 free synthetic checks in the free tier. 'Observability for Good' (nonprofits) exists but is out of scope (not a student/startup program).

### OpenObserve

- **Self-Hosted Open Source Edition (free forever)** (high) — AGPL-3.0 open-source build (single Rust binary / Docker) with the full observability stack: logs, metrics, traces, dashboards, alerts, RUM, multi-tenancy, HA. No usage charges, no per-seat/host fees, unlimited ingestion. Cost = your own infra (object storage like S3/MinIO + PostgreSQL). Latest release Oct 2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone - students, startups, enterprises; no .edu email, no company registration, no credit card, no region limit. Self-managed updates; community support only.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Download binary or use Docker from https://openobserve.ai/downloads/ 2) Set ZO_ROOT_USER_EMAIL/PASSWORD and run, or run the container 3) Point agents (OTel, Fluent Bit, Vector, Prometheus) at it 4) GUI at localhost:5080. Zero-cost to start.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry - free forever. No renewal needed.
  - *Gotchas:* AGPL-3.0 (not Apache 2.0 since Nov 2023): if you modify and offer it as a service, source-disclosure obligations can apply; unmodified self-hosted use is fine. You run your own HA/storage - ops cost is on you. Enterprise features (SSO, RBAC, federated search, QoS, audit) are not in the OSS build - those come via the free 50GB/day Enterprise self-hosted tier below.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/downloads/(alsohttps://openobserve.ai/pricing.md)
- **Self-Hosted Enterprise Free Tier (50 GB/day)** (high) — Enterprise Edition (SSO, RBAC, federated search, query management, workload QoS, audit trail, sensitive-data redaction) is free up to 50 GB/day (~1.5 TB/month) of ingestion per legal entity, with no license key required, and includes full commercial use. This is a genuine free-forever tier, not a trial.
  - *Eligibility:* Any legal entity (B2B license; personal/household use excluded). No funding cap, no .edu requirement, no credit card, no region limit. Aggregated across all deployments of the same legal entity.
  - *How to claim:* Download the O2 Enterprise build from https://openobserve.ai/downloads/ (v1.1.x, all OSes) and run it - it works without requesting a license up to 50 GB/day. No sign-up or license key needed for the free tier. Above 50 GB/day you must request a paid license via sales.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; no stated sunset date. Free-tier allowance is part of the EULA (last updated 2026-09-05), not a time-boxed promotion.
  - *Gotchas:* Spike buffer: you may exceed 50 GB/day up to 3 times per month; after the 3rd excess, search queries are BLOCKED until volume drops back under the limit or you buy a license. 'Daily Ingestion' is counted uncompressed, per 24h UTC day. EULA is B2B-only - a student's side-project company is fine, but purely personal/household use is out of scope. Monitoring/usage telemetry is enabled under the EULA.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/pricing.md;https://openobserve.ai/docs/enterprise-setup/license-and-pricing/;https://openobserve.ai/legal/enterprise-license/
- **OpenObserve Cloud 14-day free trial (no credit card)** (high) — 14-day trial on the Pay-As-You-Go (Professional) cloud plan: $0.50/GB ingested + $0.01/GB queried, unlimited users, logs/metrics/traces/RUM/session replay; included retention: metrics 15 months, logs/traces/RUM 30 days. No credit card required to start (pricing page + ToS Free Trial section). Available in 4 regions (US-West-2 Azure, US-East-2 AWS, EU Central AWS, AP South 1 AWS).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone creating a new Cloud org - students/startups alike; no .edu requirement, no CC required to begin. No region restriction beyond the 4 offered cloud regions. Signup: https://cloud.openobserve.ai/web/login?mode=signup
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at cloud.openobserve.ai 2) Create org - 14-day trial starts automatically (code defaults trial_ends_at to 14 days) 3) Ingest/query trial features free 4) Add a payment method to continue - after 14 days the org's ingestion/search/alerts/billing endpoints return 403 until you move to Pay-As-You-Go.
  - *Expiry:* Trial ends after 14 days; no extension stated; after expiry you must switch to usage-based billing (or terminate). No CC required before trial ends.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a TRIAL, not a free tier - the cloud free tier no longer exists (killed 2025-06-02, see sunset notes). At trial end you're billed $0.50/GB ingested + $0.01/GB queried for everything ingested during the trial usage model; extra non-metrics retention is $0.02/GB per additional 30-day period. Synthetic/uptime monitoring is still beta; RUM and error tracking carry separate per-unit fees in the underlying pay-as-you-go model.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/pricing.md;https://openobserve.ai/pricing/;https://openobserve.ai/legal/terms-of-service/
- **AI features: 20 free credits in preview (AI SRE Agent + AI Assistant)** (medium) — AI-powered features (Incident Management / AI SRE Agent and AI Assistant) are free during preview; each account gets 20 credits, consumed per AI operation.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Cloud account (per pricing.md); no .edu or startup requirement; credits are per account. No stated region limit.
  - *How to claim:* Create any OpenObserve Cloud account (or use the 14-day trial) and use AI SRE Agent / AI Assistant - 20 credits are preloaded; free while in preview.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to preview status - when preview ends, standard Enterprise add-on pricing applies; 20 credits are finite and do not renew automatically.
  - *Gotchas:* AI features are listed under the Enterprise feature set, so full production use may require Enterprise plan access; 'free during preview' can end without per-user advance notice. Credits are a play-quota, not a discount.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/pricing.md(AIFeaturessection,lastupdated2026-09-04)
- **OpenObserve Startup Program (50% -> 25% Cloud discount, 12 months)** (high) — Sign a 1-year OpenObserve Cloud contract and pay 50% off for the first 6 months, 25% off for the next 6 months. Discount is contingent on the 1-year contract; no cash value; cannot be extended, transferred, or renewed; standard pricing applies automatically after the 12 months. Program may be modified/terminated on 30 days' notice.
  - *Eligibility:* ALL of: total funding raised < $10M; ARR <= $1M; anticipated ingest > 100 GB/day; actively VC-backed or in a recognized accelerator program. Global (no region limit). No .edu involvement - a company program, approval at OpenObserve's sole discretion, documentation may be requested. One application per company.
  - *How to claim:* Apply on the 'Apply Now' form at https://openobserve.ai/startup/ (be ready to confirm investor/accelerator backing; a company email on your own domain). On approval, sign the 1-year Cloud contract and the tiered discount applies from the approval date.
  - *Expiry:* Discount valid for 12 months from approval (6 x 50%, 6 x 25%); not renewable. Not free - it is a discount, useful when ingest >100GB/day (at $0.50/GB that's ~$1.5k+/mo list, so real dollar value).
  - *Gotchas:* This is NOT a free offer - minimum 100GB/day ingest requirement excludes most students and pre-revenue startups; the 1-year commitment is the cost. Resale/abuse triggers termination. After month 12, full price applies unless you terminate. Verify current terms before applying - program terms can change on 30 days' notice.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/startup/
- **Education / non-profit 'special pricing' via sales (no published program)** (medium) — OpenObserve states: 'Discounts available for eligible startups, non-profits, and educational institutions' - applied through openobserve.ai/contact-sales. There is NO published education license, student tier, .edu requirement, or fixed discount amount anywhere on the official site; no dedicated /education page exists. The Enterprise EULA is explicitly B2B-only ('business, commercial, or organizational purposes only, not consumer for personal, family, or household use'), so a purely individual student gets no licensed free offer; students' realistic free path is the self-hosted OSS or 50GB/day Enterprise free tier, plus the 14-day cloud trial.
  - *Eligibility:* Institution-level (university, research lab, non-profit) via sales negotiation; criteria not published. No .edu-only free product found.
  - *How to claim:* Contact openobserve.ai/contact-sales describing the institution and use case; expect a custom quote/quote-level discount, not a self-serve free offer.
  - *Expiry:* No program, so no expiry; offers are ad-hoc quotes.
  - *Gotchas:* Because the EULA is B2B, even a favorable education quote likely binds an institution, not a student. Do not confuse this with a student free tier - none exists.
  - *Source:* https://openobserve.ai/pricing.md(SpecialPricing);https://openobserve.ai/legal/enterprise-license/
- **2025–26 change:** SUNSET: OpenObserve Cloud's free tier was DISCONTINUED effective 2025-06-02 (announcement: https://openobserve.ai/blog/june-25-pricing-policy-updates/). Moving to fully usage-based Pay-As-You-Go with no minimums; existing free-tier cloud customers got a 30-day exemption (until ~2025-07-02) and then required a valid credit card on file for usage charges. New customers instead get the 14-day no-CC trial. PR #6922 (merged 2025-07-10) confirms the code: orgs default to a 14-day trial and the free plan was removed from the billing UI. So any 'OpenObserve Cloud free tier' seen in pre-June-2025 articles is stale. PRICING CHANGE: ingestion price moved from $0.30/GB (June 2025) to $0.50/GB on the current Professional plan (pricing.md, updated 2026-09-04) - trial/usage costs are higher than 2025 docs suggest. ALSO: uptime/CDN front - OpenObserve has no CDN; uptime/synthetic monitoring (HTTP/TCP/TLS/SSH + Playwright browser checks) is in BETA in Cloud as of Aug 2026 with no separate free tier found, and RUM/session replay on cloud carry per-unit pay-as-you-go fees; nothing in the current material is a permanent free monitoring/uptime/CDN offer - the permanent-free value is entirely in the self-hosted OSS build and the 50GB/day self-hosted Enterprise free tier.

### PostHog Free Plan

- **PostHog Free Plan (universal monthly free tier) — Observability & Product Analytics** (high) — Every PostHog product ships with a monthly free allowance that resets each month; same allowance whether or not a card is on file; usage stops at the limit so you cannot be overcharged. Exact free limits (US Cloud, current as of 2026-10-22): Product Analytics 1,000,000 events; Session Replay 5,000 web + 2,500 mobile recordings; Feature Flags 1,000,000 requests (Experiments billed with feature flags); Error Tracking 100,000 exceptions; Surveys 1,500 responses; Data Warehouse Sources 1,000,000 rows + free historical syncs; Destinations 10,000 trigger events + 1M rows; AI Observability (ex-LLM Analytics) 100,000 events; PostHog AI 500 credits (~$5); Logs 10 GB ingested; Replay Vision 2,500 credits (~$25); PostHog Desktop 2,000 credits (~$20); Workflows 10,000 messages/channel; Inbox Beta 3 PRs; Emails 10,000. Free plan = 1 project, 1-year data retention (session recordings kept ~1 month), unlimited team members, community support, SSO (Google/GitHub/GitLab), API access.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to everyone — students and startups included. No .edu email required, no credit card required, no region restriction (US Cloud default; EU Cloud available). Not a trial: it is a perpetual free tier that 'renews every month... forever.'
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at app.posthog.com or posthog.com/pricing ('Get started – free'); install SDK / add tracking code. Free allowance auto-applies every month, no application or card step needed.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; the free allowance renews every month. No expiry or annual review on the base free tier.
  - *Gotchas:* It is explicitly 'not a trial, no credit card' — a true perpetual free tier, not a 14-day trial. Free plan is capped at 1 project; adding a card (Pay-as-you-go) unlocks 6 projects and 7-year retention, not a higher free tier. Beyond the free caps you pay usage-based, and you can set per-product billing limits to prevent surprise bills. Logs custom retention beyond the free allowance is charged ($0.05/GB-month).
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/pricing
- **Error Tracking free tier (Monitoring front)** (high) — Generous free tier of 100,000 exceptions/month, GA (out of beta April 2025). No credit card required; you can set billing limits. After the free 100K: tiered pricing 100k–325k $0.000370/exception, 325k–10M $0.000140/exception, 10M+ $0.000115/exception. Bill is driven by ingested $exception events (suppression rules drop future matching exceptions pre-ingestion and are not billed).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone on the PostHog free tier — students/startups, no .edu or card required.
  - *How to claim:* Included automatically in the free tier once error tracking SDK is enabled; no separate application.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual monthly free allowance; renews each month.
  - *Gotchas:* Exception autocapture on by default can over-capture; you can trim captured types client-side. Merging/resolving/suppressing existing issues does not reduce the bill; suppression only saves on future matching events.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/docs/error-tracking/pricing
- **PostHog for Startups — $50,000 in free credits** (medium) — $50,000 in free PostHog credits for 12 months for early-stage teams (page headline: '$50K in credits for early-stage teams'). Official eligibility stated on the pricing page: company younger than 2 years and total funding raised under $5M. Credits are applied on top of/within a pay-as-you-go account so usage can far exceed the free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup (not education): founded <2 years ago, <$5M total funding raised. No .edu requirement; aimed at VC/startup founders.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at posthog.com/startups ('Get started – free' → app.posthog.com/signup). The page's application/claim steps were not fully readable in this fetch; apply via the startups page. Note: the base free tier needs no card, but redeeming $50K of usage credits effectively puts you on a pay-as-you-go account (card requirement for that account not explicitly confirmed on the page).
  - *Expiry:* 12-month credit window.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for 12 months (time-boxed), not perpetual. The eligibility caps (<2 yrs, <$5M raised) mean a more mature startup is not eligible. Do not confuse with the separate Y Combinator offer below.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/startups
- **Y Combinator Startup Program — $50,000 free credits/yr, renewable** (high) — Y Combinator-backed companies get $50,000 in free PostHog credits per year, renewable while the company's total funding raised stays under $25M (separate, YC-specific program listed on the pricing page).
  - *Eligibility:* Y Combinator companies (or a company that raised via YC); total raised must stay < $25M to keep the renewal. No .edu.
  - *How to claim:* Applied through PostHog's YC/startup program (posthog.com/startups and pricing page); YC-backed teams claim the annual $50K.
  - *Expiry:* Renews annually while raised < $25M.
  - *Gotchas:* Renewal is gated on the $25M raised ceiling — once raised funding exceeds $25M the renewable credits stop. Distinct from the general <2yr/<$5M startup program.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/pricing
- **Non-profit discount (adjacent to student/startup audience)** (high) — Registered non-profits receive 15% off purchases under $25k; an additional 5% stacks on top of standard volume discounts in the $25k–$100k range. This is a discount (not a free tier), but is one of PostHog's stated offers alongside the startup discounts.
  - *Eligibility:* Registered non-profit organization (no .edu, no startup age cap).
  - *How to claim:* Noted on the pricing page; apply the discount when setting up a paid account.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing discount; no expiry stated.
  - *Gotchas:* It is a percentage discount on spend, not free usage; the free tier (finding 1) already covers most non-profit baseline needs at $0.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/pricing
- **Education / student license — no dedicated program found** (medium) — No dedicated PostHog education, student, or .edu license/program exists in the official site. A direct fetch of posthog.com/education returns 404 (no page), and the FAQ/pricing pages do not list a student-only offer. Effectively, the 'education' front is served by the universal free tier: students can use all products free (1M events, 5K replays, 100K exceptions, 10GB logs, etc.) with NO .edu email and NO credit card, so classroom/student projects are covered at $0.
  - *Eligibility:* No .edu email required, no institutional agreement, no special license — the free tier is open to any student. (No evidence of a distinct education-specific benefit.)
  - *How to claim:* Same as the free tier: sign up at app.posthog.com; no separate education application exists to claim.
  - *Expiry:* n/a (no dedicated program; rides on the perpetual free tier).
  - *Gotchas:* Because there is no dedicated education program, there is no .edu-verified perk or extended data-retention for students; students get the same 1-project / 1-year-retention free tier as everyone else.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/pricing
- **Uptime & CDN fronts — no dedicated PostHog product** (medium) — PostHog does not sell a standalone uptime-monitoring service or a CDN, so there is no free offer specifically in those two fronts. Its 'monitoring' coverage comes through the free tier's Observability stack: Error Tracking (100K exceptions free), Logs (10 GB free), Web Vitals / performance capture, and alerts. 'Monitoring' in the uptime-sense (ping/SLO uptime checks) and any CDN/edge delivery are out of scope of PostHog's product list (analytics, session replay, feature flags, experiments, error tracking, logs, surveys, workflows, AI observability, data warehouse, destinations, inbox, PostHog AI).
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no uptime or CDN product to claim.
  - *How to claim:* n/a. For uptime/CDN you would need a different vendor; PostHog's free value is in error tracking + logs + analytics, not uptime pings or a content/edge network.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Don't list a 'free uptime monitoring' or 'free CDN' as a PostHog offer — none exists. Web-vitals/performance data inside analytics is the closest monitoring feature, and it is covered under the 1M-event free analytics allowance.
  - *Source:* https://posthog.com/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes that matter for the 'free offer' claims: (1) In 2025 PostHog raised several free-tier limits — a third-party (metacto, updated 2025-10-24) reports Surveys free allowance increased from 250 → 1,500 responses/month, and Data Pipelines free quota changed to 10K events + 1M rows (previously 1M events). These 'after' values match today's official pricing page (1,500 surveys; 10K destination trigger events + 1M rows); the 'before' values are third-party (medium confidence). (2) A new AI/LLM product launched in 2025 with 100K free events/month, initially named 'LLM Analytics' (per the 2025 metacto article) and now shown on the 2026 pricing page as 'AI Observability' — i.e., the 100K free-events figure is a 2025 addition / rebrand, not part of the original 2024 free tier. (3) Context from 2024: PostHog cut session-replay and analytics-event prices, and in 2025 cut data-pipeline and survey prices — so the free tier has generally been expanding, not shrinking. (4) Error Tracking went GA (out of beta) around April 2025 with its 100K free-exceptions tier; before that it was a beta. (5) No student/education program or startup/YC program was sunset in 2025-2026: the $50K startup program (<2yr, <$5M), the YC $50K/yr program (<$25M raised), and the 15% non-profit discount all remain live on the current pricing page. (6) Tangential: 2025 self-hosting guidance changed (support for new Kubernetes/Helm installs ended) — affects self-hosted users, not the cloud free tier. Net: every 'known so far' claim (free error tracking, session replay, logs, 1M events, no credit card) is CONFIRMED and current; nothing in the free-offer set was removed, only some limits were raised.

### Prometheus

- **Prometheus self-hosted (open source)** (high) — Core offer: Prometheus is 100% open source under Apache 2.0, a CNCF graduated project. Free, unlimited, self-contained time-series DB; no pricing, tiers, or per-metric limits exist for the OSS server. Pairs with Grafana OSS (also free) for dashboards and Alertmanager for alerting. No account, no claim, no expiry; the only cost is the infrastructure you host it on (cloud compute/egress).
  - *Eligibility:* Universal: anyone (students, startups, enterprises, noncommercial or commercial). No .edu, no funding cap, no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* No claim needed. Download binary/Docker from prometheus.io/download or your package manager; deploy and point Grafana at it. Optionally use k8s (kube-prometheus-stack).
  - *Expiry:* None — Apache 2.0 license, indefinite.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: there is no trial, it is simply free. Watch your hosting/egress costs, retention (local disk is finite), HA setup (thanos/cortex/remote write adds complexity), and scale limits of a single node. Managed tiers below exist precisely to offload ops.
  - *Source:* https://prometheus.io/
- **Grafana Cloud Free plan (managed Prometheus metrics)** (high) — Per grafana.com/pricing (read today): 3 active users; up to 10k active metric series/month; 50 GB ingested/month across logs/traces/profiles; 14-day retention for metrics/logs/traces/profiles; 100k synthetics API executions + 10k browser test executions/month; 500 k6 vUser-hours; community support. No credit card required. Pro starts at $19/mo platform fee + usage (e.g., $6.50/1k series for metrics).
  - *Eligibility:* Universal, no education/startup status needed. CC explicitly not required on the free plan.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at grafana.com (Grafana Cloud), choose the Free plan, then configure your Prometheus to remote-write to the Grafana cloud ingestion endpoint — free managed Prometheus with no card.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite free plan; no expiry, no auto-conversion.
  - *Gotchas:* Free is capped at 10k active series and 50 GB ingest/month — overage pushes you to paid. Retention is short (14 days; Pro gives 13 months metrics/30 days logs). 'No credit card required' is the page's claim — verify at signup, Grafana has occasionally asked for a card in promos.
  - *Source:* https://grafana.com/pricing/
- **Grafana Labs Startup Program (Grafana Cloud credits for Prometheus logs/traces/metrics)** (high) — Per the official blog (read today): up to $100,000 in Grafana Cloud credits for 12 months OR until the next funding round, usable on any Grafana Cloud service (logs, traces, metrics incl. managed Prometheus, k6, synthetics, IRM). Ideal profile: < $10M raised and < 25 employees, but larger startups are encouraged to apply. Admission at sole discretion; accepted startups agree to be a reference customer and possible marketing collaboration. After credits expire: guaranteed 20% discount off list pricing. Announced at ObservabilityCON, 25 Sep 2024.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startups (ideal: <$10M raised, <25 employees, open to exceeding guidelines); all new Grafana Cloud customers incl. free-tier users, and Grafana OSS users moving to managed. Not aimed at students/education. Discretionary acceptance.
  - *How to claim:* Complete the 4-question application (Typeform link published in the official blog post; program web pages grafana.com/startups and grafana.com/startup-program both 404'd today — use the blog post's link or contact Grafana sales to apply).
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 12 months or until next funding round, whichever first; renewal beyond that is the 20% list-price discount, not more credits.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: this is a credits program, not a permanent free tier — after burn, you pay (less). Reference-customer/marketing strings attached. Verify the live application URL before assuming the program is still open (web page 404 today; program is 2+ years old as of 2026).
  - *Source:* https://grafana.com/blog/grafana-labs-startup-program/
- **AWS Managed Service for Prometheus free tier** (high) — Per aws.amazon.com/prometheus/pricing (read today): free tier includes 40M ingested metric samples/month, 200B query samples processed/month, and 10 GB of stored metrics — 'you can get started with Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus for free (in supported AWS Regions)'.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with an AWS account (new-account free tier terms apply); no student/education or startup status required; no CC claim beyond normal AWS account setup.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Prometheus workspace in a supported AWS region; free tier applies automatically, no enrollment.
  - *Expiry:* Free tier is ongoing (not a 12-month trial) subject to AWS free-tier policy; limits are monthly.
  - *Gotchas:* Only available in 'supported AWS Regions' — check region coverage. Limits are per-sample, so a high-cardinality scrape job can burn 40M samples fast; overage bills per sample/GB, and the 200B query-sample cap can surprise. This sample-based structure differs from the 100 GB/month + custom-label-sets limits third-party trackers historically reported for AMP (see sunset_notes).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/prometheus/pricing/
- **Datadog Free plan (including Prometheus monitoring)** (high) — Per datadoghq.com/pricing (read today): Free tier = up to 5 hosts, unlimited user accounts, 100 custom metrics allotted per host, 500 custom events/host, 5 containers per host, out-of-the-box dashboards, unlimited alerts; full-resolution data retention shown as 1 day on the current page. Prometheus is covered via Datadog's Prometheus integration (APM/infrastructure monitoring); Prometheus is not named on the pricing page's free-tier table.
  - *Eligibility:* Universal (students/startups/anyone); no .edu requirement. Credit-card policy is not stated on the page — Datadog's signup historically asks for a payment method even for the free plan, so do not assume no-CC.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at datadoghq.com, select the Free plan, install the Datadog agent or point Prometheus at Datadog's endpoint.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan is perpetual; no expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* Current page states 1-day full-resolution retention on Free (historically 14 days) — materially thinner than older marketing. The 'Start Free Trial' CTA wording conflates the Free plan with a trial; verify you're on the Free plan, not a trial with clock. CC policy unverified on the page.
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/pricing/
- **Cloudflare for Startups (CDN/uptime front; credits + free tier)** (high) — Per cloudflare.com/startups (read today): up to $350,000 in credits, valid 1 year or until consumed (no extensions), across three tiers — Tier 3: $10,000 (bootstrapped or <$1M raised, no partner needed); Tier 2: $100,000 (<$5M raised, funded by an affiliated partner, gets an account manager); Tier 1: $350,000 ($5M+ raised, partner-funded, bi-weekly architect hours + priority support). Credits cover usage-based services (Workers, Durable Objects, Workflows, Workers AI [AI capped at $50k in T1], R2/KV/D1/Queues/Vectorize [R2 capped at $10k], Pages, Images/Stream, Cache Reserve, Argo Smart Routing). Core CDN/DNS/SSL/DDoS/WAF/load-balancing are free-included in every tier regardless.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups: incorporated within last 10 years, funded up to Series B within the last 12 months, building a tech product with a live public website, active on at least one public social channel, for-profit company email matching the domain; first-time applicants only. Excludes educational institutions, consultancies, MSPs, resellers, personal blogs — not a student offer.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the program's application page at cloudflare.com/lp/startups; review typically within 48 hours; email startups@cloudflare.com for case-by-case tier upgrades (existing members under old caps).
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 12 months or until consumed, whichever first; not extendable; upgrades don't reset the clock. Free CDN/uptime tier itself is indefinite.
  - *Gotchas:* Overages bill the card on file after credits run; AI Gateway is temporarily not credit-eligible; premium products (Magic Transit, China Network, etc.) need separate contracts. Students get nothing here — Cloudflare's plain Free plan (CDN/DNS/SSL for 100 zones) is the indefinite, no-CC, free-for-all baseline.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startups/
- **Google Cloud Startup Program credits (usable on Managed Service for Prometheus)** (medium) — Per cloud.google.com/startup/ai content surfaced in search today: up to $350,000 GCP credits over 2 years — Year 1: 100% of first $100,000 (up to $250,000 for AI-first startups), Year 2: 20% of up to an additional $100,000; includes Gemini/Gemma access and expert webinars. These general credits cover GCP's Managed Service for Prometheus ingest/query costs, which is the closest thing to a GCP 'managed Prometheus' free offer.
  - *Eligibility:* VC-funded startups: pre-seed/seed raised within last 5 years, Series A within last 12 months, founded within last 5 years, not yet received more than $5,000 in GCP credits; eligibility at Google's discretion. Not a student offer (Google Cloud's $300/60-day free trial is the general alternative).
  - *How to claim:* Apply via Google Cloud's startup program application (cloud.google.com/startup) or an affiliated YC/accelerator partner; credits land as GCP billing credits usable on any service incl. Cloud Monitoring / Managed Prometheus.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured over 2 years (Y1 100%, Y2 20%); GCP free-trial alternative: 60 days.
  - *Gotchas:* Third-party models billed directly, not covered by credits; 'up to $5,000 in GCP credits already received' gate; Y2 discount rate drops to 20%. Specific GCP Managed-Prometheus free-tier numbers could NOT be verified today (pricing pages truncated/redirecting/404) — treat any per-sample free allowance figures as unconfirmed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai
- **No student/education license exists for Prometheus (negative finding)** (medium) — Prometheus has no education license, student program, or .edu offer: prometheus.io (read today) carries no pricing, education, or partner pages at all — it is an Apache-2.0 CNCF project, so the 'student offer' is simply that the software is free and unlimited for students. No Grafana, AWS AMP, Datadog, or Cloudflare education-specific tier tied to Prometheus was found on the official pages read today. Azure's Managed Prometheus service exists (GA 2025) but its pricing page would not load today, so any Azure free-tier figures are unverified rather than reported.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — absence finding. Anything claiming a 'Prometheus education discount' (e.g., boilerplate rows on discount-tracker sites) is generic vendor-template content, not a real offer.
  - *How to claim:* n/a. For students: just self-host Prometheus OSS (free) or use Grafana Cloud Free / Cloudflare's no-CC Free plan.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Discount-aggregator pages (e.g., costbench) publish 'Education Discount / Startup Program' boilerplate for Prometheus that they themselves mark as unverified — do not treat those as real programs.
  - *Source:* https://prometheus.io/

### Sentry Developer Plan

- **Sentry Developer Plan (Free) — perpetual free tier** (high) — Sentry's 'Developer (Free)' plan is free forever: 5,000 errors/mo, 5,000,000 spans/mo, 50 session replays/mo, 5 GB logs/mo, 5 GB application metrics/mo, 1 uptime monitor, 1 cron monitor, 20 metric monitors, 10 custom dashboards, 1 GB attachments, 100 Size Analysis builds, 30-day data retention/lookback, unlimited projects, data residency available. NOT included on this tier: UI Profiling, Continuous Profiling. Pricing page tagline: 'Start free on the Developer plan forever.' Free plan is one of four tiers: Developer (free), Team, Business, Enterprise.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone (open registration, no .edu email or startup status required — students and startups qualify on equal footing with everyone else). Hard cap: 1 user. No region limits stated; data residency offered. Credit card NOT required.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up free at https://sentry.io/signup (create org, add projects, paste DSN into apps). No credit card, no trial needed, no eligibility gate.
  - *Expiry:* Never — explicitly 'forever' on the pricing page; no sunset clause published.
  - *Gotchas:* Monthly quotas are hard caps: events/attachments over quota are simply not accepted (silent data loss, e.g., a single crash bug can blow 5K errors in a day). 1 user = any teammate needs Team ($29/mo or $26/mo annual) or Business ($89/mo or $80/mo annual). If you trial Business and don't upgrade, you are silently downgraded to this tier with limits on features.
  - *Source:* https://sentry.io/pricing/
- **14-day Business free trial for all new accounts** (high) — Per docs.sentry.io/pricing: 'All new Sentry accounts come with a 14-day free trial period' — the initial trial is the Business plan ('the initial 14-day Business trial'). During it you have Business-plan features (unlimited users, unlimited dashboards, SAML/SCIM, 90-day retention, etc.). Pricing page: 'try Business for 14 days with no credit card.' No dollar trial credits exist — it is a time-boxed plan trial, not a credit balance.
  - *Eligibility:* Every new Sentry org/account, one time. No .edu, startup, or region conditions stated; credit card explicitly NOT required.
  - *How to claim:* Just create a new Sentry account (sentry.io/signup). All new accounts get it automatically; no application or credit card. Upgrade via Settings > Billing if you choose to stay on Business ($89/mo monthly, $80/mo annual).
  - *Expiry:* 14 days from account creation. After it ends: 'Users who haven't upgraded by the end of their free trial will remain on the free Developer plan with limited features.' No auto-billing since no card is on file.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial is 14 days of Business features, then downgrade to the 1-user/5K-error Developer caps — if your app is error-heavy, expect dropped events post-trial. New accounts can only request the separate one-time product trial on day 15 (after this trial ends).
  - *Source:* https://docs.sentry.io/pricing/
- **One-time 14-day product trials (incl. Seer AI debugger) — unlimited events during trial** (high) — Sentry offers a one-time 14-day trial of individual products not already in your plan. Docs: 'During your 14-day product trial, you'll be able to send us an unlimited number of events' and 'You won't be billed for any data you've used during the 14-day trial period.' This includes add-on/subscription products (e.g., Seer AI debugger trial — the docs reference a dedicated Seer trial, with no published duration/eligibility beyond the general rules; Seer itself is a paid subscription, current pricing $40/active contributor/month after legacy pricing was sunset Jan 2026). No dollar-value trial credits exist.
  - *Eligibility:* Per product, once per org ('each org can only trial each product once'). Free (Developer) accounts may request at any time; new accounts from day 15; Team/Business for products outside their plan. No credit card requirement stated; no .edu/startup conditions.
  - *How to claim:* Request from the org (Settings > Billing/plan area, 'request a product trial'; Seer via 'Start Trial' in Settings). Free accounts: any time; new accounts: day 15; existing Team/Business: any time for products not in their plan.
  - *Expiry:* 14 days per product trial; strictly one-time per product per org — a second request is not possible.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Unlimited events' applies only inside the 14-day window; afterwards the product is paid or unusable. Trial-vs-free gotcha: this is a time-boxed add-on trial, not a free plan. Seer-specific trial terms aren't fully published (see sunset notes re: Seer pricing change).
  - *Source:* https://docs.sentry.io/pricing/
- **2025–26 change:** No Sentry-specific education, student (.edu), startup, or nonprofit discount program was found on any official page checked today: sentry.io/education/, sentry.io/startups/ and sentry.io/nonprofit/ are not real program pages (machine-UI 404s), and neither the current pricing docs (docs.sentry.io/pricing) nor the legacy pricing docs mention any such program. The free offers for students/startups are therefore the general-purpose ones above (Developer free plan + 14-day trials), which carry no special eligibility. 2025-2026 changes: (1) Legacy Seer pricing ($20/month + $25 event credits) stopped being offered as an add-on as of January 2026; current Seer is $40/active contributor/month — so the 'Seer free credits' offer is gone. (2) The entire pre-June-11-2024 pricing structure (including legacy 'Performance Units' for spans) was replaced by per-product quotas (errors/spans/replays/logs/metrics as separate lines) — a 2024 change, kept as context. No 2025-2026 changes to the Developer free plan or the 14-day trials were found on the current pages (verified 2026-10-22). Paid reference points: Team $29/mo monthly or $26/mo annual (50K errors/mo, 90-day retention, unlimited users); Business $89/mo monthly or $80/mo annual; Enterprise custom. Note: web search was unavailable this session (budget exhausted), so the 'no education/startup program' conclusion rests on direct page checks of sentry.io + docs.sentry.io rather than a full web sweep.

### SigNoz

- **SigNoz Cloud 30-day free trial (no credit card)** (high) — Managed SigNoz Cloud workspaces available in US, EU, or India: "Get a 30-day trial with no credit card required" (signoz.io/teams). SigNoz docs FAQ (signoz.io/docs/faqs/general): when the trial expires with no payment method added, the deployment is deleted after a 7-day grace period (with email reminders); data cannot be recovered after deletion; an expired trial can only be reactivated via support (cloud-support@signoz.io). Trial accounts are rate-limited at the ingestion side: "You are sending data at more than 100 RPS, your ingestion may be rate limited". Post-trial pricing: Teams plan from $49/month including $49 of usage; overage at $0.30/GB logs or traces and $0.10/million metric samples; retention 15 days logs/traces, 1 month metrics on base plans (longer retention: 15/30/90/180 days or 1 year for logs/traces, 1/3/6/13 months for metrics, via support, affects pricing); Enterprise from $4000/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone who signs up (no .edu, no startup criteria, no credit card during trial). Workspaces served from US, EU, India data centers. No student-only or startup-only trial variant found.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at signoz.io/teams ("Get Started - Free" CTA): create account, choose a data source, instrument with OpenTelemetry and visualize. To keep the workspace after day 30, add a credit card under Settings > Billing before the trial or 7-day grace period ends.
  - *Expiry:* 30 days from signup, then a 7-day grace period; after that the workspace and all data are deleted (irrecoverable; reactivation only via support). Trial does not renew automatically.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a trial, not a perpetual free tier. No trial ingestion cap in GB is published on any official page I fetched today - the previously circulated "~50 GB ingest" limit could NOT be confirmed; the only documented trial cap is the ~100 RPS ingestion rate limit. Adding a credit card "does not charge you during the trial period" (docs FAQ), but a card becomes required to continue use. Cancelling a subscription deletes the workspace and its data with no restore. Each additional workspace bills separately; no consolidated billing across workspaces.
  - *Source:* https://signoz.io/teams/(trialtermsdetail:https://signoz.io/docs/faqs/general/)
- **SigNoz Startup Program - $19/month for 12 months** (high) — Eligible startups pay $19/month for SigNoz Cloud for the first 12 months instead of the $49/month Teams minimum, and that $19 includes $19 of telemetry usage each month; consumption beyond the included amount is billed at standard published rates ($0.30/GB logs or traces, $0.10/million metric samples). Roughly $19 of usage equals ~63 GB of logs or traces per month. No end date or sunset is stated on the page; the program was live as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Eligibility:* All three must hold: company less than 3 years old, fewer than 30 employees, and less than $6 million raised. No .edu/student eligibility; no credit-card requirement stated; no region limit stated. The page does not document how eligibility is verified (self-declared at signup vs. reviewed).
  - *How to claim:* Go to signoz.io/startups and click the "Start your free trial" CTA (routes to the signoz.io/teams signup). The $19 rate applies for the first 12 months of an eligible company's SigNoz Cloud subscription. Confirm the startup rate on your first invoice; contact support@signoz.io if the discount is not applied.
  - *Expiry:* The $19/month rate lasts 12 months, after which standard pricing applies (Teams from $49/month including $49 of usage). Program itself has no published expiry or sunset date.
  - *Gotchas:* Includes only $19 of usage/month - a startup shipping more telemetry will get bill overages at standard rates. The startup price is anchored to the $49 Teams minimum, which was cut from $199/month to $49/month in May 2025 (see sunset notes), so the effective discount relative to the old baseline is much larger than $30/month. No documented review process or appeal path; eligibility claims are not machine-verifiable.
  - *Source:* https://signoz.io/startups
- **Self-Hosted Community Edition - free open-source observability** (high) — SigNoz is an open-source, OpenTelemetry-native platform (metrics, traces, logs in one datastore, out-of-box APM dashboards) that you can self-host free of charge: "Get started with Community Edition" (signoz.io/pricing). Install via Docker Compose/Swarm, Linux install, or Kubernetes/Helm (including AWS, GCP, AKS, DigitalOcean guides; see signoz.io/docs/install/docker, /install/kubernetes). The pricing-page FAQ "What is the difference between SigNoz Cloud (Teams) and Community Edition?" frames the difference as: self-host the community edition yourself vs. Cloud adds managed operation plus SSO & SAML support and help configuring dashboards/alerts. There are no published data caps on the self-hosted edition - you provide your own infrastructure (ClickHouse storage, OTel collector resources are your cost). Retention is configurable per-workspace in Settings > Retention Controls. The docs product FAQ describes SigNoz as a "full-stack open source APM tool" and open source alternative to SaaS APMs. OSS license name could not be verified today (GitHub pages were unreachable from this environment).
  - *Eligibility:* No eligibility requirement: no .edu email, no credit card, no company age/funding cap - anyone, including students and startups, can self-host. Practically it requires your own server/container infrastructure (Linux/Mac/containers; the team does not support Windows installs per docs FAQ, so use containers on Windows).
  - *How to claim:* No account or payment: download/deploy via Docker Compose or the Helm chart per signoz.io/docs/install/; point OpenTelemetry SDKs/collectors at your endpoint (gRPC 4317 / HTTP 4318, no ingestion key needed, TLS off by default).
  - *Expiry:* No expiry or renewal - community edition is an ongoing open-source project; no sunset date published. Ongoing cost is your infrastructure (compute/storage), not a license fee.
  - *Gotchas:* "Free" means no software license fee; you bear infrastructure, storage, query-performance and ops cost, and any egress you arrange. The OSS license text could not be re-verified today (GitHub fetches returned 404 from this environment) - verify the repo license before commercial use if that matters. Self-hosted build lacks Cloud-only add-ons like SSO/SAML and managed help; long-term retention and high RPS are constrained by your own hardware. To leave SigNoz later, OpenTelemetry-native data makes exporting out easier, but a self-hosted-to-Cloud migration is a supported (one-way) path.
  - *Source:* https://signoz.io/pricing(install:https://signoz.io/docs/install/docker/,https://signoz.io/docs/install/kubernetes/)
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Teams price cut (May 2025): SigNoz cut the SigNoz Cloud Teams minimum from $199/month to $49/month, with $49 of usage now included at that price (blog: "Cloud Teams Plan Now at $49", with 2025/05 asset in page; per-unit rates unchanged at $0.30/GB logs/traces, $0.10/mn metric samples; the $19 startup price sits off this new baseline). 2) The previously cited free-trial figure of "~50 GB ingest" could NOT be found on any official page fetched today (signoz.io/pricing, /teams/, docs FAQs, cost-control docs); the only trial limit documented today is the ~100 RPS ingestion rate threshold - treat any "~50 GB" claim as unverified. 3) No student/education license or program exists on signoz.io as of 2026-10-22 (checked pricing, homepage, /startups/, docs FAQs, and the corporate sitemap, which lists no education/academic page). 4) No perpetual free SigNoz Cloud tier - "Get Started - Free" is the 30-day trial. 5) No standalone free Uptime/CDN offering found: uptime monitoring in SigNoz is a dashboard-template feature, not a separate free SaaS tier. 6) Method note: search-engine cross-checks were unavailable this session (shared WebSearch budget exhausted); verification was done directly against official signoz.io pages, docs .md endpoints, the corporate/product sitemaps, and SigNoz's own blog. GitHub (for the OSS license name) was unreachable from this environment and is the one claim left unverified.

### Statuspage Free Plan

- **Statuspage Free Plan (free forever public status page)** (high) — Statuspage now ships under Atlassian (statuspage.io 301-redirects to atlassian.com/software/statuspage). Confirmed today on atlassian.com/software/statuspage and /software/statuspage/pricing: a Free plan exists. Exact free limits: 1 public status page, 100 subscribers, 25 components, 2 team members, 2 metrics, email + Slack + Microsoft Teams notifications, REST API access, page analytics, scheduled maintenance, incident templates, status embed, activity log, basic customization (Statuspage branding shown). Paid-only: custom domain, white-label/branding removal, uptime monitoring checks, SMS/webhook notifications, private pages, RBAC, extra metrics. Public plans: Hobby $29/mo (250 subs, 5 team, 5 metrics, custom domain), Startup $99/mo (1,000 subs, 10 team, SMS/webhook, custom CSS), Business $399/mo (5,000 subs, 25 team, custom CSS/HTML/JS, RBAC), Enterprise $1,499/mo (25,000 subs). Private-page plans from $79/mo; audience-specific pages from $300/mo. No credit card mentioned for the free tier; paid plans note 'No minimum contracts, no sign-up fees, no cancellation fees' and can be billed yearly via signed order form.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to everyone — no .edu email, no startup age/funding condition, no region limit stated on the official pages. Credit card not stated as required for the free plan (standard Atlassian Cloud free signup).
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Atlassian Cloud account (https://www.atlassian.com/try/cloud/signup?bundle=statuspage — the exact 'Get started today' link on the free-plan tab of the pricing page). Choose the Free plan; no application, no invoice. Free for all users (students and startups alike), not gated to either group.
  - *Expiry:* Free forever; no trial end, no renewal step.
  - *Gotchas:* 1-status-page cap on the free tier (single public page; multiple pages/private pages are paid). Uptime/monitoring checks are NOT in the free tier — free is page-subscription only; monitoring requires a paid plan. The paid plan literally named 'Startup' ($99/mo) is a price tier, not a startup discount — easy to confuse. Free-tier pages keep Statuspage/Atlassian branding (white-label is paid). If you later add paid apps, everything bills through the Atlassian Cloud bill (unified billing).
  - *Source:* https://www.atlassian.com/software/statuspage/pricing
- **Atlassian for Startups — 12 months of Atlassian Premium products at $0 (up to 50 users) — does NOT cover Statuspage** (high) — Atlassian's official startup program (Atlassian for Startups) — free for 12 months for up to 50 users, 'Premium edition of our products for $0'. Confirmed today on atlassian.com/software/startups which products: Jira Premium (50 seats), Confluence Premium (50), Bitbucket Premium (50), Loom Business + AI (50), Jira Service Management & Customer Service Management (10 seats), Jira Product Discovery Premium (10 creator seats + unlimited contributors). Jira Product Discovery is the exception to the 50-seat cap. Statuspage is NOT listed as a covered product, and the FAQ adds 'The list of products may be modified over time.' Marketplace apps excluded. Governed by Atlassian Customer Agreement Section 17 as 'Free or Beta Products'.
  - *Eligibility:* ALL three criteria: (1) not an existing Atlassian paid customer; (2) VC funded OR associated with a partner accelerator/incubator; (3) has not raised more than US$10M in external funding. Bootstrapped startups without VC/accelerator association are NOT eligible.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at https://www.atlassian.com/software/startups ('Get it free' CTA); Atlassian reviews every application and may request proof of funding (e.g., Crunchbase/Pitchbook profile). VCs, incubators and accelerators share the offer with portfolio companies by emailing startups@atlassian.com.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing program; each eligible startup enrolls for ONE 12-month period. Before the 12 months end you can renew or cancel any included product; going past 50 users (or 10 for the service-collection apps) requires a paid upgrade/quote.
  - *Gotchas:* Bootstrapped, no-VC startups fail criterion 2 — the most common disqualifier. Covers Atlassian Premium editions of core apps only: Statuspage / Statuspage Monitoring are not in the product list you get, so a startup using Statuspage under this program would still pay Statuspage pricing (or rely on the Statuspage Free Plan). 'Startup' as a Statuspage $99/mo plan name is unrelated to this program. After 12 months the apps are not automatically downgraded — you renew or cancel explicitly.
  - *Source:* https://www.atlassian.com/software/startups
- **Atlassian Classroom Licenses (education license for students & teachers)** (medium) — Atlassian's education offer: 'Classroom Licenses are designated for use by students & teachers within a classroom setting. Fill out our application form today!' — the only public education program I could confirm today (found via Atlassian's sitemap; atlassian.com/teams/education and /education are 404). The page is a survey/application form; no published eligibility numbers, .edu-verification text, or duration appear on the page. No mention of Statuspage anywhere on the classroom-licenses page (Statuspage appears in the site navigation only).
  - *Eligibility:* Students and teachers in a classroom/educational setting; application-based (institution/school context implied by the form). No .edu requirement or regional limit stated on the page. Effectively aimed at K-12/higher-ed classrooms, not for students as individuals or for startups.
  - *How to claim:* Fill out the application at https://www.atlassian.com/solutions/survey/classroom-license-request (the page's survey form). The official page does not publish seat caps, terms or product coverage beyond the one-line classroom designation — terms come back from Atlassian after review.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page (form-based; Atlassian reviews and responds).
  - *Gotchas:* This is Atlassian's general classroom-license program for its core apps — the page contains zero Statuspage references, so assume Statuspage pages are NOT covered; verifying actual coverage requires submitting the form. A page found via the sitemap only — the main education marketing pages (404) suggest this offer is low-visibility and possibly legacy.
  - *Source:* https://www.atlassian.com/solutions/survey/classroom-license-request
- **No trial credits / student pricing / monitoring trial found for Statuspage paid tiers** (medium) — As of today, atlassian.com/software/statuspage/pricing shows no free trial of the paid tiers (Hobby $29 / Startup $99 / Business $399 / Enterprise $1,499 public; Starter $79 / Growth $249 / Corporate $599 private), no trial-credit or education-price blocks, and no '14-day trial' of monitoring. The only 'credit card' mention is the FAQ 'Do you offer yearly credit card billing?' (yes, manual via signed order form). Web search was unavailable (session budget exhausted), so absence-of-trial reflects the official pages read today, not a search of reseller/marketplace promos.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A
  - *How to claim:* No claim path: no trial-credits offer, no student pricing, no trial CTA on the current pricing page. Paid plans are bought through the Atlassian Cloud bill (annual billing via signed order form, per the pricing-page FAQ).
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse the Statuspage 'Startup' plan ($99/mo) with a startup program, and do not assume the free 12-month Atlassian-for-Startups premium entitlement applies to Statuspage (it does not list Statuspage). Atlassian Cloud signups do not require a credit card, but paid Statuspage tiers do add recurring charges to the Atlassian bill.
  - *Source:* https://www.atlassian.com/software/statuspage/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes confirmed today: (1) statuspage.io and statuspage.io/pricing now 301-redirect to atlassian.com/software/statuspage — the standalone Statuspage domain, pricing page and legacy product site are retired; the product is fully absorbed into Atlassian's cloud offering (acquisition completed 2023). (2) docs.statuspage.io redirects to an /inactive page — the old documentation hub is sunset; docs now live at support.atlassian.com/statuspage. (3) atlassian.com/ondemand (the former OnDemand/monitoring marketing home) now redirects to /software. (4) atlassian.com/teams/education, /education and /training/teaching all 404 — the old education program pages are gone; only the sitemap-listed classroom-license-request form remains as the education offer. (5) Atlassian for Startups' covered product list has shifted over time (now Jira/Confluence/Bitbucket Premium, Loom Business+AI, JSM & Customer Service Management, Jira Product Discovery — Opsgenie no longer listed); the page (dateModified 2026-10-21) warns 'The list of products may be modified over time', and Statuspage has not been a covered product.

### Uptime Kuma

- **Uptime Kuma software itself: free MIT license, unlimited monitors & status pages (self-hosted)** (high) — Uptime Kuma is free open-source (MIT) self-hosted monitoring: $0 license, no tiers, no vendor, no monitor caps. Unlimited monitors, multiple public status pages, ~20-second check intervals, 90+ notification channels (Slack, Discord, Telegram, email, webhooks, Pushover, Apprise), 2FA, SSL certificate expiry alerts. Free identically for students, startups, and enterprises. The only cost is hosting: a small VPS (~$5-20/mo per third-party pricing pages) or $0 on an existing always-on box. No .edu email needed, no region limits, no credit card. Funding of the project itself is via GitHub Sponsors/Open Collective (noted special sponsor Atlas Cloud, USD 400/month) - this is optional support, not a purchase requirement. Verified on the official repo today: the page mentions no pricing page, no commercial tier, no cloud service, no education or startup program.
  - *Eligibility:* Universal - any person or company, including students and startups; no .edu email, no age/funding cap, no region restriction, credit card NOT required (nothing to purchase).
  - *How to claim:* No claim step: git clone / download, run the Docker image louislam/uptime-kuma (or bare-metal Node.js >= 20.4 with PM2) on any server; no sign-up, no account, no credit card. A full install via Docker Compose takes ~5-15 minutes. Optional community plugins via the built-in plugin marketplace.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; MIT license grants rights without time limit. No renewal, no trial countdown. Note v2.0.0 (released 2025-10-20) was a major breaking upgrade (rootless Docker images, removed legacy JSON backup/restore, LiquidJS templating) - existing instances must migrate; the software remains free.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: there is no trial - the entire product is permanently free; the paid components are third-party hosting/support, never the software. Data/egress: you pay your VPS host, and misconfigured shared cloud (e.g. Oracle Cloud always-free idle policy, which took down the maintainer's own instance in 2023) can silently stop the monitor. Operational cost: TLS, backups, upgrades, and security are on you; a single self-hosted instance monitors from one location and cannot monitor its own host. NFS filesystems are not supported. The public demo at demo.kuma.pet is temporary (data wiped every 10 minutes) and is not a hosted offering.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/louislam/uptime-kuma
- **Third-party managed-hosting free tiers/trials for Uptime Kuma (not student/startup programs)** (medium) — No Uptime Kuma first-party hosting exists, so any 'hosted free Uptime Kuma' comes from third-party PaaS/VPS vendors offering generic free tiers or short trials: Elestio (managed, $20 in trial credits valid 3 days, then hourly credit billing, free level-1 support), Stellar Hosted (14-day free trial; paid plans EUR 49-199+/mo in Amsterdam EU), DANIAN (7-day free trial, no credit card, from EUR 9/mo, 21 regions), Enzonic (free 'Wood-adjacent' tier: 812 MB RAM, 100% CPU, 10 GB NVMe, no credit card, always-on, subject to availability). Kuma Engine (kumaengine.com, third-party LLC, terms updated 2025-07-01) offers fully-managed Uptime Kuma at US$4/mo or US$24/yr with 5 GB block storage, 5 GB monthly transfer, and 5,000 SMTP email notifications - NO free tier. None of these target students or startups with special eligibility.
  - *Eligibility:* Generic end users; none require .edu email, startup age, or a funding cap. Age 18+ per Kuma Engine ToS. No region limits on Elestio/DANIAN; Stellar is EU (Amsterdam). Credit card NOT required on Enzonic free tier, DANIAN trial, and Elestio trial; Kuma Engine requires prepaid payment.
  - *How to claim:* Generic (not student/startup-specific) trials: sign up at the host, deploy the Uptime Kuma instance from its catalog. E.g. Elestio: create instance, use $20 trial credits over 3 days (then pay hourly credits; level-1 support free). Stellar Hosted: 14-day free trial on the 100%-money-back-guarantee plans. DANIAN: 7-day free trial, no credit card, then from EUR 9/mo. Enzonic: permanent free tier (812 MB RAM / 100% CPU / 10 GB SSD, no card) that runs Uptime Kuma always-on, subject to availability - the closest thing to a $0 hosted path.
  - *Expiry:* Trials: Elestio $20 credits valid 3 days; Stellar 14 days; DANIAN 7 days. Enzonic free tier: 'free forever' but subject to capacity availability. Kuma Engine: none - paid only, auto-renewing, non-refundable.
  - *Gotchas:* These are hosting offers, not Uptime Kuma offers - terms change with the host, not the project. Enzonic's free tier is capped at ~812 MB RAM and can be reclaimed; Elestio credits expire after 3 days and you keep paying hourly after; Kuma Engine's 5,000 SMTP email cap and 5 GB egress can throttle heavy alerting. Do not confuse the temporary public demo (kuma.pet) with a free hosted instance. All third-party plans disclaim SLA unless explicitly stated.
  - *Source:* https://elest.io/open-source/uptime-kuma/resources/plans-and-pricing
- **MIT-licensed Uptime Kuma software, free for anyone (no student or startup program)** (high) — Uptime Kuma is released under the MIT License (GitHub LICENSE: Copyright (c) 2021 Louis Lam). Downloading, using, modifying and self-hosting it cost $0 (Doolpa review, Pricing Starts At $0) and GBP 0 (uptimekuma.io licence line). The license imposes no paid tier, feature gating or monitor-count cap, and no monitor-count cap appears in the official sources read. Official Docker image: louislam/uptime-kuma:2. Minimum check interval: 20 seconds (README feature list; Doolpa review). Non-Docker install needs Node.js >= 20.4 and Git; pm2 is recommended for background running. Platforms per README: major Linux distros and Windows 10 x64 / Windows Server 2012 R2 or higher. README lists FreeBSD, OpenBSD, NetBSD, Replit and Heroku as unsupported, and NFS storage as unsupported.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone. Checked for gates: no .edu or school email requirement, no startup age or funding cap, no credit card, and no region limits, because the MIT license and the official README contain none of these. It is not a student- or startup-specific program, so there is nothing to apply for.
  - *How to claim:* No sign-up or claim step. Get the software from https://github.com/louislam/uptime-kuma and run it on your own server. Docker: docker run -d --restart=always -p 3001:3001 -v uptime-kuma:/app/data --name uptime-kuma louislam/uptime-kuma:2, or curl the repo compose.yaml and run docker compose up -d. Non-Docker: git clone the repo, run npm run setup, then node server/server.js or pm2 start server/server.js --name uptime-kuma.
  - *Expiry:* None. MIT has no expiry or renewal and applies to every copy of the software with no time limit.
  - *Gotchas:* (1) uptimekuma.io is NOT the project author's site. Its footer and licensing page name SMARTX TECHNOLOGIES Ltd (London), which sells paid managed hosting. The homepage shows a plan reported at 4.2 PLN per month (figure as summarized by the fetch tool, not independently checked). The homepage's free subdomain (uptimekuma.eu), free SSL and daily backups are bundled into that paid plan and are not a standalone free tier. Its 'unlimited monitored services' wording describes the paid plan, not the software. (2) uptimekuma.co is an unofficial fan site that disclaims affiliation with the author. GitHub metadata lists uptime.kuma.pet as the homepage; per the README it is a temporary demo server whose data is deleted after 10 minutes. It returned HTTP 403 to WebFetch. A demo is not an account, trial or credit. (3) Free means the software is free, not the hosting: you pay for the server, backups, updates and admin time. The uptimekuma.io March 2026 marketing article estimates GBP 5 per month for a small UK VPS and GBP 60 to 120 over 12 months for a self-hosted server. That is a vendor estimate, not an offer. (4) Because README lists Replit and Heroku as unsupported, those free app platforms cannot host it.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/louislam/uptime-kuma/blob/master/LICENSE
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22, Uptime Kuma has no student-specific, education, or startup offers, and never has - it is MIT-licensed software with a $0 license for everyone, so no vendor cap, trial, or discount program exists to sunset. 2025-2026 changes: (1) v2.0.0 shipped 2025-10-20 as the project's biggest release - breaking changes (rootless Docker images, LiquidJS SMTP templates replacing regex-based ones, removed legacy JSON backup/restore, UI refresh); the 2.3.x line (through v2.3.2, 2026-05-03) added OracleDB monitor support and SQLite-locking fixes, and 2.5.x extends push-interval limits beyond ~24 days - still all free. (2) No first-party cloud/managed service launched; 'Uptime Kuma hosting' listings are third parties (Kuma Engine LLC since at least 2025 at $4/mo per-instance with no free tier; Stellar Hosted, DANIAN, Elestio, Enzonic, ElfHosted, DreamHost pre-installed VPS). (3) Project funding remains volunteer-based: GitHub Sponsors / Open Collective, with Atlas Cloud as a noted special sponsor (USD 400/month). (4) No free-hosting sunset in 2025-2026 detected; the oldest known host-side gotcha is Oracle Cloud's always-free idle-instance policy (2023), which still affects Kuma self-hosters on that tier. The official site (uptime.kuma.pet) blocks automated fetchers (403), so pricing/program verification relies on the GitHub repo plus third-party pages. Confidence: high on absence of official offers (read the official repo today); medium on third-party trial details (from host pages, not tested).

### UptimeRobot Free Plan

- **Free Plan (50 monitors, forever free)** (high) — Official pricing page (fetched 2026-10-22): $0/month. 50 monitors; 5-minute check interval; HTTP/website, keyword, ping, port, API, UDP, dependency, location-specific, slow-response, DNS and SSL/domain-expiry monitor types; 1 basic status page (UptimeRobot subdomain, no custom domain/white-label); 3-month data retention; only 5 integrations allowed; no login seats, no notify seats; monthly email reports only first 3 months; no SMS/voice credits included (pay-per-pack, from $3 one-time for 10). No credit card required. Free for any use including commercial and business use under current terms.
  - *Eligibility:* Everyone: no .edu email, no startup age/funding cap, no region limit, no credit card. Commercial use allowed again per terms updated 2026-05-26; governed by a Fair Use Policy (no coordinated multi-account circumvention, no automation patterns straining shared infrastructure; enforcement discretionary).
  - *How to claim:* Register free at uptimerobot.com (pricing page 'Register now'); no credit card. Paid upgrades optional via 'Upgrade' in header/My Settings.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — permanent free tier, renews indefinitely while within 50-monitor/5-min limits and Fair Use Policy. Downgrade/lapse of any paid plan reverts account to this plan and pauses monitors above the 50 limit.
  - *Gotchas:* 2024-12-01 ToS had banned commercial use; that ban was reversed in the 2026-05-26 terms update (stale third-party guides still claim it). SMS/voice alerts are NOT free: one-time credit packs ($3/10 msg, $15/100, ...; Russia/Az/BD etc. consume 2-5 credits per SMS, voice calls 2 each). 3-month log retention is short; API is rate-limited (~10 req/min per third-party reporting — verify in-app). No maintenance windows, no Slack/Webhook/PagerDuty on free (5 integrations only).
  - *Source:* https://uptimerobot.com/pricing/
- **Sponsored Plan for non-profits, charities & open-source projects** (medium) — Official help article (updated 2026-09-28): non-profit, charity, or open-source projects that outgrow the free plan 'may be eligible for a Sponsored Plan that provides premium features at no cost'. No published spec of included features or monitor counts; case-by-case via support.
  - *Eligibility:* Representatives of non-profits, charities, or open-source projects needing more than the free plan. Not a student/startup program — no .edu requirement mentioned, no funding-cap criteria published. Adjacent, not aimed at for-profit startups.
  - *How to claim:* Contact the UptimeRobot support team; they state eligibility criteria and application steps on request. No public application form found on official pages.
  - *Expiry:* Not published; treated as a review-based sponsorship, so renewal/duration unknown — confirm terms in writing when granted.
  - *Gotchas:* Eligibility and scope are opaque until you apply; 'premium features at no cost' is not itemized, so verify which plan (Solo/Team/Scale equivalents) is covered. For-profits/startups are not the stated audience — a funded startup would need the free plan or paid plans.
  - *Source:* https://help.uptimerobot.com/en/articles/11604710-who-should-use-uptimerobot-s-free-plan
- **AI-agent sign-up flow (free account, no signup form)** (medium) — Announced in official blog (updated 2026-07-29): an AI agent can create the free account and a live HTTPS monitor with no traditional registration. Agent submits site URL + user's email (after solving a proof-of-work anti-abuse puzzle); user clicks 'Activate' in the confirmation email; monitor goes live on the free plan (50 monitors, 5-min checks, 1 public status page, 5 integrations incl. Discord/Google Chat/Pushover/Pushbullet/Splunk, iOS/Android apps).
  - *Eligibility:* Same as the Free Plan — any use including commercial; email required; no credit card, no .edu, no region limit. Available via any agent that follows UptimeRobot's llms.txt / agent-skills instructions.
  - *How to claim:* Ask your agent: "Set up uptime monitoring for <site> with UptimeRobot's agent flow. My email is <email>"; then click Activate in the inbox confirmation. Nothing is created until you confirm.
  - *Expiry:* Same as Free Plan — permanent, no trial clock; subject to the 50-monitor/5-min Fair Use limits.
  - *Gotchas:* This is an onboarding channel, not extra quota: it yields the identical free-plan limits, so don't double-count it against the main Free Plan finding. Confirmation email must be opened or the monitor never starts.
  - *Source:* https://uptimerobot.com/blog/your-ai-agent-can-now-set-up-uptime-monitoring-no-signup-required/
- **No dedicated education license, student program, or startup program** (medium) — Verified against the official help article (2026-09-28): there is no education license, .edu-verified student offer, startup trial credit, or startup accelerator partnership. Students and startups are simply listed as audiences for whom the standard Free Plan fits ('educational institutions monitoring course platforms and student resources', 'startups and side projects'). The only beyond-free cost-free offer is the non-profit/OSS Sponsored Plan above. Web-search budget was exhausted mid-research; no additional official program page was located on uptimerobot.com or help.uptimerobot.com.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — absence finding. Free Plan requires no .edu; startups require no funding-age criteria because no separate program exists.
  - *How to claim:* Students/startups claim the standard Free Plan (register free at uptimerobot.com, no credit card). Nothing extra to apply for.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not assume a student discount exists elsewhere; any 'education' framing on third-party sites is marketing, not a UptimeRobot program.
  - *Source:* https://help.uptimerobot.com/en/articles/11604710-who-should-use-uptimerobot-s-free-plan
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Commercial-use ban on the Free Plan (introduced in the Oct 2024 ToS update, enforced 2024-12-01) was REVERSED in the terms updated 2026-05-26 and re-confirmed by the 2026-06-15 help article: the free plan is again explicitly available for business/commercial use. Stale third-party content (e.g., Spork's 2026-03 and StillUp's 2026-05 write-ups) still claims the ban is in force. 2) Paid lineup was re-priced and restructured in 2026: earlier articles cite Solo $7-8/mo and Team $29-34/mo; current US pricing is Solo $13/mo ($12 annual), Team $46/mo ($39 annual), new 15-second Scale plan $98/mo ($79 annual) with 200/500-monitor tiers, Enterprise custom — EU visitors see euro pricing (e.g., Solo €10/mo). Team moved to 30-second checks. 3) No student/education or startup-specific program ever existed to sunset; the only cost-free non-standard offer (Sponsored Plan for non-profits/OSS) remains active with no announced change. 4) New 2026: AI-agent sign-up channel for free accounts (proof-of-work + email confirmation gates). Note: a session-wide web-search budget was exhausted partway through this research, so absence claims rest on the official pricing + help pages fetched today plus full-text search snippets.
